Critical

Attackers Exploit Arista VeloCloud Orchestrator Command Injection Flaw

The Hacker News
2026-07-28 9 use cases 3 techniques 5 kill-chain phases detected

A maximum-severity security flaw impacting on-premises versions of Arista VeloCloud Orchestrator (VCO) has come under active exploitation in the wild. The vulnerability, tracked as CVE-2026-16812 (CVSS score: 10.0), is a case of operating system command injection that could pave the way for arbitrary code execution. Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution · Email attachment opened from external sender
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] Arista VeloCloud Orchestrator (CVE-2026-16812) OS command-injection payload in web requests · Office app spawning script/LOLBin child process · Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha)
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Outbound C2/exfil to Arista VeloCloud CVE-2026-16812 attacker infrastructure · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] Post-exploitation shell/recon spawned by VeloCloud Orchestrator web/Java service
Arista VeloCloud Orchestrator (CVE-2026-16812) OS command-injection payload in web requests AIExploitation Medium
Hunts inbound HTTP requests to the VeloCloud Orchestrator (VCO) web interface that carry OS command-injection signatures (subshell/backtick/separator + recon command) or originate from Arista-published attacker IPs. Targets the unauthenticated command-injection vector (CVE-2026-16812) that reaches privileged internal VCO functionality via crafted GET/POST requests. Rationale: Uses the three Arista-published attacker IPs plus command-injection signatures (subshell $()/backtick, URL-encoded %24%28/%60, separator-then-recon-command) grounded in the confirmed unauthenticated OS-command-injection vector delivered over HTTP GET/POST. Corroborating write-ups confirm attackers scanned for exposed orchestrator endpoints and delivered payloads via HTTP requests. Cross-checked against: • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/arista-patches-velocloud-orchestrator-zero-day-exploited-in-attacks/ • https://www.securityweek.com/critical-arista-velocloud-orchestrator-vulnerability-exploited-as-zero-day/ • https://www.theregister.com/security/2026/07/28/arista-patches-actively-exploited-velocloud-bug-as-cisa-puts-admins-on-the-clock/5279414
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.004
Data sourcesWeb.Web Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Legitimate API calls whose query strings contain semicolons or pipes (matrix params, base64 payloads with '+/=')
  • Security scanners / authorized pentest of the VCO generating injection-style probes
  • Automation tooling passing shell-like arguments through URL parameters
  • WAF already blocking the request (DeviceAction=blocked) — still logged but not a compromise
Suppression filter
| where not (DeviceAction in ("blocked","deny","dropped"))
Response runbook
1) TRIAGE: Pull the full request/response for the matched RequestURL and confirm the target host is a VCO on-prem instance (versions <5.2.3.14/6.1.3.4/6.4.2.4/7.0.0.1). Check DeviceAction/status — a 200 to an injection payload is a likely hit; note SourceIP. 2) CONTAIN: Block the three Arista IOC IPs and any confirmed attacker source at the perimeter, restrict the VCO web interface to trusted admin networks, and snapshot/preserve VCO web-access, backend, system and DB logs before remediation. 3) HUNT: On the VCO host review admin activity and pivot to UC 'post-exploit process' and 'outbound C2 to IOC IPs'; rotate VCO and managed Edge credentials if command execution is confirmed.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteIP in ("8.19.75.217","206.72.242.124","206.72.242.162")
| where LocalPort in (443, 80, 8443, 9443)
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, RemoteIP, RemotePort, LocalIP, LocalPort, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc
CommonSecurityLog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where isnotempty(RequestURL)
| extend Payload = strcat(RequestURL, " ", tostring(RequestContext), " ", Message)
| where SourceIP in ("8.19.75.217","206.72.242.124","206.72.242.162")
    or Payload matches regex @"(?i)(\$\(|`|%60|%24%28|;\s*(id|whoami|uname|cat|curl|wget|bash|sh|nc)\b|\|\s*(id|whoami|uname|cat|curl|wget|bash|sh|nc)\b|%3b|%7c|&&|\|\|)"
| project TimeGenerated, DeviceVendor, DeviceProduct, SourceIP, DestinationIP, RequestMethod, RequestURL, Message
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Arista VeloCloud Orchestrator CVE-2026-16812 Command Injection in Web Request
id: 2f5c1e7a-9b4d-4c62-8a1e-6d3f0b2c7e11
description: Detects OS command-injection signatures or Arista-published attacker source IPs in HTTP requests to the VeloCloud Orchestrator web interface (CVE-2026-16812).
references:
  - https://thehackernews.com/2026/07/attackers-exploit-arista-velocloud.html
  - https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/arista-patches-velocloud-orchestrator-zero-day-exploited-in-attacks/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/08/03
tags:
  - attack.t1190
  - attack.t1059.004
logsource:
  category: webserver
detection:
  selection_ioc:
    c-ip:
      - '8.19.75.217'
      - '206.72.242.124'
      - '206.72.242.162'
  selection_inject:
    cs-uri-query|contains:
      - '$('
      - '%24%28'
      - '%60'
      - ';id'
      - ';whoami'
      - '|whoami'
      - '|id'
      - '&&'
      - '%3B'
      - '%7C'
  condition: selection_ioc or selection_inject
level: high
APPLICATION
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats summariesonly=true count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Web.Web where (Web.src IN ("8.19.75.217","206.72.242.124","206.72.242.162") OR Web.uri_query="*$(*" OR Web.uri_query="*%24%28*" OR Web.uri_query="*%60*" OR Web.uri_query="*;id*" OR Web.uri_query="*;whoami*" OR Web.uri_query="*|whoami*" OR Web.uri_query="*|id*" OR Web.uri_query="*&&*" OR Web.uri_query="*%3B*" OR Web.uri_query="*%7C*") by Web.src Web.dest Web.http_method Web.uri_path Web.uri_query Web.status | `drop_dm_object_name(Web)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)` | sort - count

Outbound C2/exfil to Arista VeloCloud CVE-2026-16812 attacker infrastructure AICommand & Control Medium
Alerts on any network connection to/from the three Arista-published IPs tied to CVE-2026-16812 exploitation. Highest-fidelity pivot: these are dedicated attacker IPs, so a callback from the VCO host (or any internal host) indicates active compromise, C2, or data exfiltration of SD-WAN/orchestrator data. Rationale: Directly uses the three attacker IPs Arista published as responsible for the attacks (8.19.75.217, 206.72.242.124, 206.72.242.162). Dedicated-infrastructure IOC match on network telemetry is the single cleanest signal; Arista explicitly urged customers to block these and review logs, and to check for unexpected outbound activity from the VCO host. Cross-checked against: • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/arista-patches-velocloud-orchestrator-zero-day-exploited-in-attacks/ • https://www.securityweek.com/critical-arista-velocloud-orchestrator-vulnerability-exploited-as-zero-day/
ATT&CKT1071 T1041
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • An IP later reassigned to a benign service after the campaign (stale IOC — re-validate age)
  • Threat-intel enrichment/sandbox appliance in the org intentionally probing the IPs
  • Perimeter firewall logging the block as an allowed-log event (DeviceAction shows denied — not a compromise)
  • Shared-hosting IP where an unrelated legit service also resides
Response runbook
1) TRIAGE: Identify the internal host in the connection and whether it is the VCO or a managed device; check DeviceAction (allowed vs blocked) and SentBytes/ReceivedBytes for exfil volume. An allowed session from the VCO to an IOC IP = confirmed compromise. 2) CONTAIN: Isolate the affected host, block all three IOC IPs bidirectionally, and preserve VCO web/backend/system/DB logs and filesystem timestamps before any rebuild. 3) HUNT: Rotate VCO and managed VeloCloud Edge credentials, validate managed-device state, review admin activity for unexpected changes, and pivot to post-exploit process execution on the VCO; consider restoring the orchestrator from a trusted pre-compromise source.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteIP in ("8.19.75.217","206.72.242.124","206.72.242.162")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, LocalIP, LocalPort, RemoteIP, RemotePort, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc
CommonSecurityLog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where DestinationIP in ("8.19.75.217","206.72.242.124","206.72.242.162") or SourceIP in ("8.19.75.217","206.72.242.124","206.72.242.162")
| project TimeGenerated, DeviceVendor, DeviceProduct, SourceIP, DestinationIP, DestinationPort, DeviceAction, SentBytes, ReceivedBytes, Message
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Network Connection to Arista VeloCloud CVE-2026-16812 Attacker Infrastructure
id: 8c1d4a63-2e9f-4b71-b0c5-1a7e9d2f4c88
description: Detects connections to the three Arista-published IP addresses associated with active exploitation of VeloCloud Orchestrator CVE-2026-16812.

  category: network_connection

detection:

    DestinationIp:
      - '8.19.75.217'
      - '206.72.242.124'

level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:3 @DestinationIp:(8.19.75.217 OR 206.72.242.124 OR 206.72.242.162)

| stats count(*) as flows, sum(bytes) as totalBytes by srcAddr, dstAddr, dstPort, action
| sort totalBytes desc

Post-exploitation shell/recon spawned by VeloCloud Orchestrator web/Java service AIActions on Objectives Medium
Hunts child shell or reconnaissance processes (sh/bash, whoami, id, uname, curl, wget) spawned by the VCO's web/application service (java, nginx, httpd, node, python) — the expected result of CVE-2026-16812 command injection executing OS commands under the service context. Article-specific because VCO runs a Linux app stack and the flaw yields arbitrary OS command execution. Rationale: Grounded in the confirmed outcome of CVE-2026-16812 — arbitrary OS command execution on the Linux-based VCO host under the web/application (java/nginx) service context. Web-server-parented shells plus recon binaries (whoami/id/uname) are the textbook post-injection signal; corroborating reporting confirms attackers leveraged command execution to establish persistence. Cross-checked against: • https://www.rescana.com/post/active-exploitation-alert-arista-velocloud-orchestrator-cve-2026-16812-command-injection-vulnerability-enables-remote-co • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/arista-patches-velocloud-orchestrator-zero-day-exploited-in-attacks/
ATT&CKT1059.004 T1033 T1082
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • VCO built-in health checks / self-monitoring scripts invoking curl to localhost (filtered)
  • Vendor update or supervisor scripts where java/python legitimately shell out during maintenance
  • Backup or log-rotation cron jobs spawning shell utilities
  • Admin troubleshooting on the appliance console spawning id/uname interactively
Suppression filter
| where not (ProcessCommandLine has_any ("127.0.0.1","localhost","/opt/vco/health","logrotate"))
Response runbook
1) TRIAGE: Inspect the child ProcessCommandLine and parent command line — recon (whoami/id/uname) or outbound curl/wget to an external host from the web/java service is high-signal. Confirm the host is a VCO and correlate the timestamp with UC1 injection hits and UC2 IOC-IP callbacks. 2) CONTAIN: Isolate the VCO, kill the offending process tree, block the IOC IPs, and preserve process, web and system logs before remediation. 3) HUNT: Look for follow-on persistence (crontab writes, new SSH keys, added users), credential access to /etc/velocloud config/DB, and lateral movement to managed VeloCloud Edge devices; rotate VCO and Edge credentials and rebuild the orchestrator from a trusted source if confirmed.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("java","nginx","httpd","apache2","node","python","python3","supervisord")
| where FileName in~ ("sh","bash","dash","whoami","id","uname","curl","wget","nc","ncat","perl","chmod","crontab")
| where not (ProcessCommandLine has_any ("127.0.0.1","localhost"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, FolderPath
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("java","nginx","httpd","apache2","node","python")
| where ActingProcessName has_any ("/sh","/bash","/dash","whoami","/id","uname","curl","wget","/nc","ncat","perl","chmod","crontab")
| where not (ActingProcessCommandLine has_any ("127.0.0.1","localhost"))
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: VeloCloud Orchestrator Web Service Spawning Shell or Recon Process (CVE-2026-16812)
id: b7a2f309-4d18-4e5c-9f21-3c6a8e1b0d47
description: Detects Unix shell or reconnaissance binaries spawned by the VeloCloud Orchestrator web/application service, indicating command execution via CVE-2026-16812.
references:
  - https://thehackernews.com/2026/07/attackers-exploit-arista-velocloud.html
  - https://www.rescana.com/post/active-exploitation-alert-arista-velocloud-orchestrator-cve-2026-16812-command-injection-vulnerability-enables-remote-co
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/08/03
tags:
  - attack.t1059.004
  - attack.t1033
  - attack.t1082
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: linux
detection:
  selection_parent:
    ParentImage|endswith:
      - '/java'
      - '/nginx'
      - '/httpd'
      - '/apache2'
      - '/node'
      - '/python'
      - '/python3'
  selection_child:
    Image|endswith:
      - '/sh'
      - '/bash'
      - '/dash'
      - '/whoami'
      - '/id'
      - '/uname'
      - '/curl'
      - '/wget'
      - '/nc'
      - '/ncat'
  condition: selection_parent and selection_child
level: high
source:linux.auditd @auditd.type:EXECVE @process.name:(sh OR bash OR dash OR whoami OR id OR uname OR nc OR ncat OR curl OR wget OR perl OR chmod OR crontab)
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 ParentBaseFileName=/^(java|nginx|httpd|apache2|node|python[0-9.]*)$/i
| ImageFileName=/\/(sh|bash|dash|whoami|id|uname|curl|wget|nc|ncat|perl|chmod|crontab)$/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ParentBaseFileName, ImageFileName, CommandLine], function=count())

ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-16812", "CVE-2025-68686", "CVE-2026-16723")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-16812", "CVE-2025-68686", "CVE-2026-16723")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes

The Generator Can't Be the Validator: What OpenAI's Hugging Face Incident Proves About AI Security

Snyk
2026-07-28 1 use case 2 techniques 5 kill-chain phases detected

OpenAI’s Hugging Face incident is a wake-up call: AI systems can escape their own test harnesses, and vendors can’t be the only ones validating safety.

ATT&CKT1190 T1204.002
Click any ATT&CK pill below to open it on the Matrix

Command & Control

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution · Email attachment opened from external sender
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Office app spawning script/LOLBin child process · Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha)
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement
Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
Email attachment opened from external sender Delivery High
Attachment delivery + execution by recipient — most common malware initial access.
ATT&CKT1566.001 T1204.002
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Endpoint.Processes EmailAttachmentInfo DeviceProcessEvents
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MalAttachments = EmailAttachmentInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | project NetworkMessageId, RecipientEmailAddress,
              AttachmentFileName = FileName, AttachmentSHA256 = SHA256;
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("OUTLOOK.EXE","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                      "mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe")
| join kind=inner MalAttachments on $left.AccountUpn == $right.RecipientEmailAddress
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, AttachmentFileName, AttachmentSHA256
// Sentinel doesn't have an attachment-hash table. Best approximation:
// OfficeActivity Send/Receive activity + correlate to host process spawn.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MailWithAttach = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation in~ ("Send","Receive")
    | where isnotempty(MessageId)
    | project EmailTime = TimeGenerated, MailboxOwnerUPN, Sender,
              Recipients, Subject, MessageId;
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName endswith "OUTLOOK.EXE"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"

          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Office Application Spawning Script Host
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d07
status: experimental
description: |
    Word/Excel/Outlook/OneNote spawning powershell, cmd, wscript,
    cscript, mshta, rundll32, regsvr32 — classic macro / embedded-
    object payload signature.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1566/001/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059
    - attack.t1566.001
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_parent:
        ParentImage|endswith:
            - '\winword.exe'
            - '\excel.exe'
            - '\powerpnt.exe'
            - '\outlook.exe'
            - '\onenote.exe'
            - '\mspub.exe'
            - '\visio.exe'
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
    condition: selection_parent and selection_child
falsepositives:
    - Legitimate automation macros in trusted internal documents
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe","outlook.exe","onenote.exe","mspub.exe","visio.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe","outlook.exe","onenote.exe","mspub.exe","visio.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
   or (FileName =~ "wmic.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "/node:")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |
    PsExec, paexec, smbexec, csexec, remcom — admin-grade tooling that
    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1021.002
    - attack.t1569.002
    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_psexec:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'
    selection_wmic_remote:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\wmic.exe'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/node:'
    condition: selection_psexec or selection_wmic_remote
falsepositives:
    - Sysadmins running approved remote-management tooling — wrap with
      a known-admin-host watchlist
level: high
ENDPOINT

       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha) Exploitation High
Browser-pasted PowerShell launched from explorer/Run dialog — ClickFix social engineering.
ATT&CKT1204.004 T1059.001
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("explorer.exe","RuntimeBroker.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","mshta.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(iex|invoke-expression|frombase64|downloadstring|hxxp|curl |wget )"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName endswith "\explorer.exe"
   or ParentProcessName endswith "\RuntimeBroker.exe"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\pwsh.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
| where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(iex|invoke-expression|frombase64|downloadstring|hxxp|curl |wget )"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: FakeCaptcha / ClickFix - Clipboard-Injected PowerShell from Explorer
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d06
status: experimental
description: |
    "FakeCaptcha" / "ClickFix" social-engineering: malicious site asks
    user to press Win+R and paste a payload. The result is
    powershell/mshta launched directly from explorer.exe with iex /
    base64 / downloadstring in the command line.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1204/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059.001
    - attack.t1204.002
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_parent:
        ParentImage|endswith:
            - '\explorer.exe'
            - '\RuntimeBroker.exe'
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
    selection_cmd:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - 'iex'
            - 'invoke-expression'
            - 'frombase64'
            - 'downloadstring'
            - 'hxxp'
            - 'curl '
            - 'wget '
    condition: selection_parent and selection_child and selection_cmd
falsepositives:
    - IT-driven scripted automation that admins paste-launch
level: high

Dysphoria IoT Botnet Adds Blockchain C2 and Victim Relays After JackSkid Disruption

The Hacker News
2026-07-27 10 use cases 3 techniques 5 kill-chain phases detected

Dysphoria, an Internet of Things (IoT) botnet line tracked by CNCERT and XLab, has adopted blockchain-based name services and infected-device relays after a March law-enforcement operation against JackSkid infrastructure. The researchers say the design makes the botnet harder to disrupt. CNCERT, China's national computer emergency response team, and XLab, the threat-intelligence lab of Chinese

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution · Email attachment opened from external sender
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] IoT propagation via CVE-2017-17215 Huawei HG532 command injection (Dysphoria spreading) · Office app spawning script/LOLBin child process · Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha)
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] IoT/edge host resolving blockchain (ENS/SNS) name-service C2 infrastructure — Dysphoria · [LLM] Dysphoria victim-relay heartbeat to login.trees4sale.net:9000 · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
IoT/edge host resolving blockchain (ENS/SNS) name-service C2 infrastructure — Dysphoria AICommand & Control High
Hunts hosts resolving the Dysphoria campaign's Ethereum/Solana name-service records (burrberry.eth, 24carnforth2merseyside.sol, ukranianhorseriding.eth) or the public blockchain RPC/gateway domains the bot uses to resolve them. IoT gear, cameras, routers and servers have no legitimate reason to perform ENS/SNS resolution, making this the headline 'blockchain C2' behaviour. Rationale: Uses the second-source-corroborated ENS/SNS names burrberry.eth and 24carnforth2merseyside.sol (Security Affairs) plus ukranianhorseriding.eth, and the public blockchain resolver infrastructure the bot must reach to resolve them — a behaviour with near-zero legitimate footprint on IoT/OT/server estates. Corroborated against Security Affairs and BleepingComputer; the 5 THN-listed IPs were dropped as no second source confirmed them. Cross-checked against: • https://securityaffairs.com/196182/malware/dysphoria-botnet-uses-blockchain-domains-to-hide-c2-infrastructure.html • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/new-dysphoria-ddos-botnet-spreads-to-200k-devices-worldwide/ • https://thehackernews.com/2026/07/dysphoria-iot-botnet-adds-blockchain-c2.html
ATT&CKT1568 T1071.001
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Developer or DevOps workstations calling Infura/Alchemy RPC for legitimate dApp work
  • Crypto-wallet browser extensions resolving *.eth via eth.limo/eth.link
  • Web3 CI/CD pipelines or blockchain node software on servers
  • Security researchers detonating samples in a lab segment
  • Threat-intel enrichment tools resolving IOC domains
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe","node.exe")
Response runbook
1) Triage: identify the host class — an IP camera, router, DVR or Linux server resolving *.eth/*.sol or blockchain RPC is almost never legitimate; confirm the device role and whether any crypto/web3 software is authorised. 2) Contain: isolate the device from the internet (block egress to the resolver domains and any subsequently returned IPs at the firewall), and if it is IoT/OT that cannot run EDR, quarantine its VLAN. 3) Hunt: pivot on the client IP for connections to login.trees4sale.net:9000 (relay heartbeat) and for inbound Telnet/SSH brute-force or CVE-2017-17215/CVE-2025-9528 exploitation that preceded the resolution; sweep the subnet for peer devices showing the same DNS pattern.
let CampaignLabels = dynamic(["burrberry.eth","24carnforth2merseyside.sol","ukranianhorseriding.eth","m3rnbvs5d.eth"]);
let BlockchainResolvers = dynamic(["eth.limo","eth.link","cloudflare-eth.com","mainnet.infura.io","api.mainnet-beta.solana.com","solana-mainnet.g.alchemy.com","eth-mainnet.g.alchemy.com","rpc.ankr.com"]);

| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| summarize FirstSeen=min(Timestamp), LastSeen=max(Timestamp), Hits=count(), SampleUrl=any(RemoteUrl) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, RemoteUrl, RemotePort
| order by FirstSeen desc

Dysphoria victim-relay heartbeat to login.trees4sale.net:9000 AICommand & Control High
Hunts the Dysphoria relay-only build reporting in to its heartbeat/collection server login.trees4sale.net on TCP 9000 — the infrastructure that turns a compromised device into a covert traffic relay in the botnet's victim-relay mesh. This is the concrete network signature of the post-JackSkid resilience mechanism. Rationale: login.trees4sale.net:9000 is the relay heartbeat/collection endpoint named by Security Affairs' independent write-up of the same XLab/CNCERT research — a hard, single-purpose IOC with essentially no benign use, so any resolution or connection is high-fidelity. Port 9000 is carried through as the non-standard-port secondary signal. Cross-checked against: • https://securityaffairs.com/196182/malware/dysphoria-botnet-uses-blockchain-domains-to-hide-c2-infrastructure.html • https://thehackernews.com/2026/07/dysphoria-iot-botnet-adds-blockchain-c2.html
ATT&CKT1090.001 T1571
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Sinkhole or threat-intel platform deliberately resolving the IOC domain
  • Passive-DNS or brand-monitoring tooling enumerating the domain
  • Analyst detonating a sample in a monitored lab segment
  • Proxy/DNS-filter appliance logging a blocked lookup (verify DeviceAction was a block, not allow)
Response runbook
1) Triage: confirm the resolution/connection was allowed (not blocked at proxy/firewall) and identify the source device; a relay build drops DDoS modules, so expect an unremarkable host suddenly bridging traffic. 2) Contain: block trees4sale.net and TCP 9000 egress at the perimeter, isolate the device, and disable UPnP/port-mapping on the local gateway (the relay uses UPnP to open NAT pinholes). 3) Hunt: on the same device look for the blockchain name-service resolution (UC1) that seeds the relay list, unexpected inbound connections it is now proxying, and enumerate other internal hosts resolving trees4sale.net to size the relay mesh.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| extend u = tolower(RemoteUrl)
| where u has "trees4sale.net"
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| order by Timestamp desc
union isfuzzy=true
(
  CommonSecurityLog
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
  | where DestinationHostName has "trees4sale.net" or DestinationDnsDomain has "trees4sale.net" or RequestURL has "trees4sale.net" or (DestinationPort == 9000 and DestinationHostName has "trees4sale")
  | project TimeGenerated, Src=SourceIP, Dst=DestinationIP, DestinationHostName, DestinationPort, DeviceProduct, Activity
),
(
  DnsEvents
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
  | where Name has "trees4sale.net"
  | project TimeGenerated, Src=ClientIP, Name, Result
)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

IoT propagation via CVE-2017-17215 Huawei HG532 command injection (Dysphoria spreading) AIExploitation Medium
Hunts inbound exploitation of the Huawei HG532 UPnP/TR-064 command-injection flaw (CVE-2017-17215) — a spreading vector confirmed for Dysphoria — via the /ctrlt/DeviceUpgrade_1 endpoint and NewStatusURL/NewDownloadURL SOAP shell injection on TCP 37215. Targets the initial-access step that re-infects IoT gear after the JackSkid takedown. Rationale: CVE-2017-17215's exploit shape is a fixed, well-documented signature — POST to /ctrlt/DeviceUpgrade_1 with shell metacharacters inside NewStatusURL/NewDownloadURL over TCP 37215 — and Security Affairs corroborates it as a Dysphoria propagation vector, so the URI/port pattern is concrete rather than a generic brute-force template. Defender/Falcon left empty because inbound IoT exploitation is not visible to endpoint EDR (IoT gear runs no agent); it lives on WAF/IDS/firewall telemetry. Cross-checked against: • https://securityaffairs.com/196182/malware/dysphoria-botnet-uses-blockchain-domains-to-hide-c2-infrastructure.html • https://thehackernews.com/2026/07/dysphoria-iot-botnet-adds-blockchain-c2.html
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.004
Data sourcesWeb
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Legitimate ISP/CPE remote management using TR-064 on TCP 37215
  • Internal vulnerability scanners (Nessus/Qualys) probing the DeviceUpgrade_1 endpoint
  • Penetration-test or red-team IoT assessment traffic
  • Shodan/Censys internet-wide scanning hitting exposed devices
Suppression filter
| where ipv4_is_private(SourceIP) == false and DestinationPort == 37215
Response runbook
1) Triage: confirm the request came from an external/untrusted source IP and whether the SOAP body carried shell metacharacters (busybox/wget/tftp) versus a benign TR-064 call; identify the targeted device model and its exposure. 2) Contain: block inbound TCP 37215 and the source IP at the perimeter, and take the affected CPE/router off the internet — patch or replace if end-of-life. 3) Hunt: if the target device is reachable, check it for the follow-on blockchain name-service resolution (UC1) and relay heartbeat to trees4sale.net:9000 (UC2) that indicate successful infection, and sweep for the same 37215/DeviceUpgrade_1 pattern across the estate to map the spreading wave.
CommonSecurityLog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where (RequestURL has "/ctrlt/DeviceUpgrade_1")
    or (DestinationPort == 37215 and (RequestURL has "NewStatusURL" or Message has "NewStatusURL"))
    or (Message has "DeviceUpgrade_1" and Message has_any ("busybox","wget","tftp","/bin/","NewStatusURL","NewDownloadURL"))
| project TimeGenerated, SourceIP, DestinationIP, DestinationPort, RequestMethod, RequestURL, DeviceProduct, DeviceVendor, Activity, Message
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-9528")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-9528")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```

    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url

| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]

        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd

title: FakeCaptcha / ClickFix - Clipboard-Injected PowerShell from Explorer
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d06
status: experimental
description: |
    "FakeCaptcha" / "ClickFix" social-engineering: malicious site asks
    user to press Win+R and paste a payload. The result is
    powershell/mshta launched directly from explorer.exe with iex /
    base64 / downloadstring in the command line.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1204/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059.001
    - attack.t1204.002
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_parent:
        ParentImage|endswith:
            - '\explorer.exe'
            - '\RuntimeBroker.exe'
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
    selection_cmd:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - 'iex'
            - 'invoke-expression'
            - 'frombase64'
            - 'downloadstring'
            - 'hxxp'
            - 'curl '
            - 'wget '
    condition: selection_parent and selection_child and selection_cmd
falsepositives:
    - IT-driven scripted automation that admins paste-launch
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("explorer.exe","RuntimeBroker.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","mshta.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*iex*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*hxxp*" OR Processes.process="*curl*" OR Processes.process="*wget*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("explorer.exe","RuntimeBroker.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","mshta.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*iex*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*hxxp*" OR Processes.process="*curl*" OR Processes.process="*wget*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Critical

Public Exploit Released for Patched vBulletin Pre-Auth Code Execution Flaw

The Hacker News
2026-07-27 8 use cases 3 techniques 3 kill-chain phases detected

Public exploit details released on July 27 show how an unauthenticated request can reach PHP's eval() function inside vBulletin and execute code on an unpatched forum server. The attack requires no account, administrative access, or interaction from another user. SSD Secure Disclosure lists vBulletin 6.2.1 and earlier, and 6.1.6 and earlier, as affected, but does not give a lower version

Exploitation. Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
vBulletin CVE-2026-61511 pre-auth RCE via ajax/render/pagenav pagenav[pagenumber] payload AIExploitation High
Hunts inbound HTTP requests to the public vBulletin route ajax/render/pagenav carrying a pagenav[pagenumber] value that is operator-heavy or unusually long — the exact vector the public PoC uses to reach runMaths()->eval(). Normal pagination sends a bare integer; a 'phpfuck' payload is built from parentheses, XOR, concatenation and other operators with no letters. Rationale: Anchors on the exact article-named vector: route ajax/render/pagenav + parameter pagenav[pagenumber] carrying non-numeric arithmetic/XOR/concatenation operators (the phpfuck alphabet). A benign paginator only ever sends an integer, so operator characters in that specific parameter are near-unambiguous exploitation. Confirmed against SSD, BleepingComputer and Karma(In)Security. Cross-checked against: • https://ssd-disclosure.com/vbulletin-runtime-template-runmaths-preauth-rce/ • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/vbulletin-fixes-critical-pre-auth-rce-flaw-with-public-exploit/ • https://karmainsecurity.com/KIS-2026-13
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesWeb.Web
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Security scanners / vuln management (Nessus, Qualys, Nuclei) firing the CVE-2026-61511 template against the forum
  • Penetration-test or red-team validation of the patch
  • WURFL/analytics plugins that append bracketed query params (rarely with math operators)
  • A researcher manually reproducing the PoC on a staging forum
  • URL-fuzzing tools (ffuf, wfuzz) spraying operator-heavy payloads across many params
Suppression filter
| where not(RequestClientApplication has_any ("Nessus", "Qualys", "Nuclei", "ffuf", "wfuzz", "zgrab")) and SourceIP !in ("<approved scanner IPs go here at deploy>")
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull the full RequestURL and SourceIP; URL-decode the pagenav[pagenumber] value and confirm it contains phpfuck operators (parentheses/^/|/~) rather than a digit, and check the HTTP response code/size for signs the eval() returned command output. 2) Contain: block the SourceIP at the WAF/edge, and if the forum build is < 6.2.2 (or a pre-Patch-Level-1 6.2.1/6.2.0/6.1.6), take it offline or virtual-patch the ajax/render/pagenav route until upgraded. 3) Hunt: pivot to web-server process telemetry for php/httpd/nginx/w3wp spawning shells (UC2), review file writes to webroot for dropped webshells, and search other internet-facing vBulletin hosts for the same SourceIP or payload shape.
CommonSecurityLog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where RequestURL has "ajax/render/pagenav"
| where RequestURL has_any ("pagenav%5Bpagenumber%5D", "pagenav[pagenumber]")
| where RequestURL matches regex @"(?i)pagenav(%5B|\[)pagenumber(%5D|\]).{0,300}(%28|\(|%5E|\^|%7C|\||%7E|~)"
| project TimeGenerated, DeviceVendor, DeviceProduct, SourceIP, DestinationHostName, RequestMethod, RequestURL, RequestClientApplication, DeviceAction
| order by TimeGenerated desc

vBulletin web server (php/httpd/nginx/w3wp) spawning a shell — CVE-2026-61511 post-exploitation AIExploitation High
Detects a web-application service process (php-cgi/php-fpm/php, httpd/apache2, nginx, w3wp) spawning a command interpreter or download utility — the direct post-exploitation signature of CVE-2026-61511, where runMaths() rebuilds PHP's system() to run an OS command. Web servers almost never legitimately spawn cmd/powershell/bash/curl/wget. Rationale: Article states the PoC 'rebuilds PHP's system function and run an operating-system command', so a vBulletin worker process (php-fpm/httpd/nginx/w3wp) spawning a shell or curl/wget is the highest-fidelity execution signal. Parent-process constraint (web worker) plus child interpreter is a near-zero-baseline pairing on a forum host. Removed the invalid IsInitiatingProcessRemoteSession clause per validation feedback. Cross-checked against: • https://ssd-disclosure.com/vbulletin-runtime-template-runmaths-preauth-rce/ • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/vbulletin-fixes-critical-pre-auth-rce-flaw-with-public-exploit/
ATT&CKT1059.001 T1059.004 T1190
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Legitimate PHP cron/maintenance scripts shelling out to curl/wget for feed fetches
  • Backup or deployment tooling launched under the web-service account
  • Admin plugins that invoke system utilities (image processing via ImageMagick 'convert', clamscan)
  • Monitoring agents run as the web user executing whoami/uname health checks
  • Composer / package updates spawning shells during a maintenance window
Suppression filter
| where not(ProcessCommandLine has_any ("clamscan", "convert", "composer", "newrelic", "datadog-agent") or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has "cron")
Response runbook
1) Triage: capture the child ProcessCommandLine and confirm it is attacker-driven (recon like whoami/id/uname, or download of a second stage) rather than an app-maintenance script; correlate the timestamp against UC1 web hits from the same host. 2) Contain: isolate the forum host, kill the offending process tree, and revoke/rotate any credentials readable by the web-service account. 3) Hunt: enumerate files written under the webroot for dropped webshells, review outbound connections from the web-service account for C2, and check for persistence (new cron jobs, modified vBulletin templates/plugins).

| project TimeGenerated, Computer, SubjectUserName, ParentProcessName, NewProcessName, CommandLine

Unpatched vBulletin inventory audit for CVE-2026-61511 (< 6.2.2 / pre-Patch-Level-1) AIReconnaissance Medium
Hunting sweep that surfaces vBulletin installations still on affected builds (6.2.1/6.2.0/6.1.6 and earlier without Patch Level 1, i.e. anything below 6.2.2) so they can be prioritised for patching, since the live risk is concentrated in self-hosted internet-facing forums that have not updated. Article gives exact version boundaries. Rationale: Article names the exact affected/fixed boundary (fixed 6.2.2 released July 1; affected 6.2.1/6.2.0/6.1.6 and earlier without Patch Level 1) and stresses that risk concentrates in unpatched self-hosted forums, making a version-inventory hunt directly actionable. Marked Medium/hunting because third-party web-app version data depends on the asset/TVM catalogue actually inventorying vBulletin. Cross-checked against: • https://ssd-disclosure.com/vbulletin-runtime-template-runmaths-preauth-rce/ • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/vbulletin-fixes-critical-pre-auth-rce-flaw-with-public-exploit/
ATT&CKT1190
Data sources
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Forum on 6.2.1/6.2.0/6.1.6 that already has Patch Level 1 applied but reports the base version string (patched-but-flagged)
  • Staging/dev vBulletin instances not internet-facing (lower real risk)
  • Version string parsed from a plugin or bundled library rather than the core build
  • TVM lag where the version was upgraded to 6.2.2 but inventory hasn't re-synced
  • Custom/forked vBulletin builds with non-standard version numbering
Response runbook
1) Triage: for each flagged host confirm the true running build (admincp version page or file hash of /includes/vb5/template/runtime.php) and whether Patch Level 1 was applied to a 6.2.x/6.1.6 branch. 2) Contain: for genuinely unpatched internet-facing forums, schedule an emergency upgrade to 6.2.2 (or apply the branch patch) and put a virtual patch / WAF rule on the ajax/render/pagenav route in the interim. 3) Hunt: on any host confirmed vulnerable and exposed, retro-hunt UC1 web logs and UC2 process telemetry for exploitation during the ~4-week window between the late-June patch and the July 27 public PoC.
